# Proposed OEIS comments: A279801 to A295412

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A279801

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279801>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [622](../papers/0501-1000/0622-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) -64*a(n-2) -8*a(n-3) +72*a(n-4) -88*a(n-5) +144*a(n-6) +288*a(n-7) -112*a(n-8) -32*a(n-9) -16*a(n-10) -384*a(n-11) -256*a(n-12)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 196 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 13. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280553>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [679](../papers/0501-1000/0679-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) - 12*a(n-2) + 5*a(n-3) + 12*a(n-4) - 12*a(n-5) - 3*a(n-6) + 6*a(n-7) - a(n-9) for n>12.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(13 + 322*x - 928*x^2 + 21*x^3 + 930*x^4 + 341*x^5 - 79*x^6 - 480*x^7 - 148*x^8 - 205*x^9 - 238*x^10 + 6*x^11) / ((1 - x)^3*(1 - x - x^2)^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 13 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 49 155 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [403](../papers/0001-0500/0403-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) -32*a(n-2) +56*a(n-3) -48*a(n-4) +16*a(n-5) +4*a(n-7) -20*a(n-8) +26*a(n-9) +18*a(n-10) -66*a(n-11) +46*a(n-12) -14*a(n-14) +14*a(n-15) -4*a(n-16) -12*a(n-17) -5*a(n-18) +25*a(n-19) -10*a(n-20) -2*a(n-21) -a(n-22) -5*a(n-23) +10*a(n-24) -2*a(n-25) -2*a(n-26) -a(n-28) +a(n-29) for n>37

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 8 194 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 37. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281473

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281473>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2519](../papers/2501-3000/2519-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - a(n-4) + a(n-5) -a (n-6) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 + 232*x - 379*x^2 + 189*x^3 - 251*x^4 + 124*x^5 - 288*x^6 + 132*x^7 - 24*x^8 + x^9 + 2*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [8093](../papers/8001-8500/8093-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 6*a(n-2) + 8*a(n-3) - 5*a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5) - 2*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(7 + 13*x + 3*x^2 - 3*x^3 - 2*x^5) / (1 - 2*x - 6*x^2 - 8*x^3 + 5*x^4 - 2*x^5 + 2*x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 20 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A283039

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283039>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [203](../papers/0001-0500/0203-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 72 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 867 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 72, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A283723

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283723>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [183](../papers/0001-0500/0183-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 80 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 370 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 80, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 + 12*x + 18*x^2 + 18*x^3 + 67*x^4 + 11*x^5 - 50*x^6 - 22*x^7) / (1 - 6*x - 28*x^3 - 3*x^4 - 49*x^5 - 33*x^6 + 34*x^7 + 22*x^8). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 22 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
